- **proxima**: The proxima-native worker. Already understands proxima's pipe algebra, the sans-IO tier discipline, the runtime model (prime/tokio), and where the source-of-truth docs live — so it works in-bounds by default instead of being spoon-fed the conventions. Use for any build/change/explain task in proxima that isn't already covered by a more specialized proxima-* agent (architect, critic, judge, debugger, security, integrator, migrator, test-writer, bencher, concentrator, teacher). - **proxima-architect**: Designs proxima primitives — traits, sans-IO state machines, APIs, config/builder surfaces — inside the workspace guiding principles. Use as the AUTHOR or SYNTHESIZER role in a research-rigor tournament on a proxima design decision, or whenever a new primitive's shape is the question. Produces concrete Rust signatures with a principle cited per decision, not prose. NOT for implementing an already-settled design (use the proxima agent or proxima-migrator), NOT for critiquing someone else's design (use proxima-critic), NOT for explaining what already exists (use proxima-teacher). ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-architect.md))
- **proxima-bencher**: Builds a new performance-sensitive primitive under the disciplined-component gate — feature-flagged default-off, explicit comparison baselines, mandatory micro-benches per component (multi-size, adversarial, and a home-turf incumbent arm), CoV-tracked, alloc-counted, with a versioned discipline log where every tweak's delta is recorded including rollbacks. Use for /disciplined-component work and for any perf claim that needs a number behind it. NOT for diagnosing a correctness bug (use proxima-debugger), NOT for designing the primitive's shape (use proxima-architect). ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-bencher.md))
- **proxima-concentrator**: Concentrates one concept's full vertical slice down onto its canonical primitive — decides which primitive is the floor, deletes the over-expansion above it, repoints every code layer and every doc at it. Use when a concept is duplicated, over-expanded, or name-drifted across the stack: a type built on a pipe that did not need to exist, a trait restating a capability trait, one name meaning almost-but-not-quite the same thing in N places. Triggers on "concentrate X into the primitives", "point this down", "collapse this onto the primitive", "make the docs point down". Behaviour-preserving. NO… ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-concentrator.md))
- **proxima-critic**: Adversarial design critic for proxima primitives. Given a design candidate and the task it answers, finds every weakness — principle violations, missed requirements, wrong assumptions, uncovered edge cases, composability failures — and proposes NO fixes. Use as the CRITIQUE role in a research-rigor tournament, or whenever a design needs an unsparing attack before it lands. NOT for ranking candidates (use proxima-judge), NOT for producing an alternative (use proxima-architect), NOT for auditing crypto (use proxima-security). ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-critic.md))
- **proxima-debugger**: Diagnoses hard bugs in proxima by INSTRUMENTING and reading actual execution payload data — never by guessing or reading code alone. Works one failing case at a time, reverse-engineers from what SHOULD exist, and lands on a specific file:line or a specific missing data row with the execution data that proves it. Use for root-cause-loop work, intermittent failures, and bugs where a first read did not yield the cause. NOT for a mechanical bug you can already see (fix it directly), NOT for aggregate performance analysis (use proxima-bencher). ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-debugger.md))
- **proxima-gate**: Read-only verification runner for the slot-0 workspace. Runs builds, tests, clippy, rustdoc, greps and git inspection in a named worktree and reports the numbers. Does NOT edit, commit, or fix. Use for every "is this green", "what does this grep return", "run the gate" task — it exists so the main loop never runs cargo or grep itself. ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-gate.md))
- **proxima-integrator**: Lands a diverged feature branch onto main safely. Rebases for linear history, reconciles conflicts by incumbent-main-wins plus apply-the-branch's-feature-intent, validates GREEN, and stops before the fast-forward for human verification unless the task explicitly authorizes landing. Works only in an isolated worktree; never touches main's checkout, never pushes without authorization. Figures conflicts out from the code and the branch's intent rather than stopping to ask. NOT for propagating a known mechanical change (use proxima-migrator), NOT for collapsing a concept onto a primitive (use pro… ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-integrator.md))
- **proxima-judge**: Independent judge for research-rigor tournaments on proxima design. Given the task and 2-3 labeled candidates (plus a critique as auxiliary context, never as a candidate), ranks them best-to-worst for Borda aggregation, scoring on proxima-binding axes — sans-IO compliance, RISC/minimality, no_std, correctness and coverage, composability, teaching — never on prose quality. Runs fresh per round. NOT for producing or fixing a design (use proxima-architect), NOT for enumerating weaknesses (use proxima-critic). ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-judge.md))
- **proxima-migrator**: Propagates a mechanical API or dependency change across a crate and its entire call chain — tokio→prime/offload, param-form→associated-type Pipe, a renamed or re-signatured API, an edge-keying change. Maps the blast radius first, threads the new form through EVERY caller, and validates GREEN. Behaviour-preserving: the existing behaviour is the oracle. Works in an isolated worktree, never pushes, never leaves a half-migrated tree. NOT for deciding what the new shape should be (use proxima-architect), NOT for rebasing a branch (use proxima-integrator), NOT for judging what earns its existence (… ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-migrator.md))
- **proxima-security**: Composition-flaw auditor for crypto and protocol code — handshake and auth FSMs, AEAD, anti-replay, KDF, obfuscation codecs, address validation. Finds the bugs no dependency scanner catches: nonce reuse, role asymmetry, padding oracles, key-derivation mistakes, frame-atomicity violations, side-channel timing, state confusion. Reports findings by severity with the precise composition flaw, why it bites, and the fix. Use for a whole-crate security audit or a security review of a diff. NOT for transitive-dep CVEs (cargo audit covers those), NOT for general code quality. ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-security.md))
- **proxima-teacher**: Authors TEACHING documentation — curriculum, tutorials, algebra and concept explainers, example READMEs, book chapters — for a named reader who does NOT already know the codebase. Use for "write a doc that teaches X", "explain the pipe algebra", "make this concept land", "our doc drifted from the code, reteach it". NOT doc-writer (that is terse, why-only, reference-shaped). NOT proxima-concentrator (that collapses a concept and repoints existing docs; this one authors the doc that teaches it).… ([brianbruggeman/proxima](https://github.com/brianbruggeman/proxima/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/proxima-teacher.md))

The brief carries the role prompt and any read-only constraint. ([benkraus/pstack-omp](https://github.com/benkraus/pstack-omp/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-panel-b.md) · ★1)
- **pstack-panel-b**: Panel seat B for pstack arena runners, architect runners, interrogate reviewers, and the arena cross-judge. Pin a distinct model per seat via /pstack:setup-pstack for model diversity. ([Haaris-7/pstack-devin-version](https://github.com/Haaris-7/pstack-devin-version/blob/HEAD/pstack/agents/pstack-panel-b.md))
- **pstack-panel-c**: pstack's panel seat C, one of three model-diverse seats (default family xAI or Google). Use when a pstack skill fans out a panel, such as arena runners, architect runners, interrogate reviewers, cross-judges, and model races. Spawn one subagent per seat. The brief carries the role prompt and any read-only constraint. ([benkraus/pstack-omp](https://github.com/benkraus/pstack-omp/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-panel-c.md) · ★1)
- **pstack-panel-c**: Panel seat C for pstack arena runners, architect runners, interrogate reviewers, and the arena cross-judge. Pin a distinct model per seat via /pstack:setup-pstack for model diversity. - **pstack-panel-d**: Panel seat D for pstack arena runners, architect runners, interrogate reviewers, and the arena cross-judge. Pin a distinct model per seat via /pstack:setup-pstack for model diversity. - **pstack-parent**: pstack worker on the session model. Use for roles set to auto or inherit-parent. ([mr-jones123/pstack-harness](https://github.com/mr-jones123/pstack-harness/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-parent.md))
- **pstack-perf**: pstack delegate for performance work. Model role: perf-issue. Internal agent used by poteto-mode; select it by role rather than by hand. ([tanabe1478/pstack-opencode](https://github.com/tanabe1478/pstack-opencode/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-perf.md))
- **pstack-perf-issue**: Bounded performance implementation ([punk-dev-robot/dotfiles](https://github.com/punk-dev-robot/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/config/custom/pi/agent/agents/pstack-perf-issue.md) · ★1)
- **pstack-perf-issue**: Pstack perf role: trace measured slowness against a baseline ([marcelormendes/poteto-pi](https://github.com/marcelormendes/poteto-pi/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-perf-issue.md))
- **pstack-poteto-agent**: Pstack delegate for a task owned by a poteto-mode coordinator. Reads the bundled pstack mode and works within the assigned scope. ([dmae97/omk](https://github.com/dmae97/omk/blob/HEAD/packages/coding-agent/resources/pstack/agents/pstack-poteto-agent.md) · ★153)
- **pstack-poteto-agent**: Bounded implementation in Poteto style ([punk-dev-robot/dotfiles](https://github.com/punk-dev-robot/dotfiles/blob/HEAD/config/custom/pi/agent/agents/pstack-poteto-agent.md) · ★1)
- **pstack-poteto-agent**: Delegate subagent for code-writing and helper tasks spawned during a `/pstack-poteto-mode` run. Use for delegates, not as the runner for `/pstack-poteto-mode` itself. Spawn a fresh `pstack-poteto-agent` for each new task, and resume one only in the strict cases that pstack-poteto-mode's Subagents section names. Reads the `pstack-poteto-mode` skill's `SKILL.md` in full before any work, including its inline Principles index. Substituting `generalPurpose` skips that read and drifts. ([sergical/pstack-skills](https://github.com/sergical/pstack-skills/blob/HEAD/skills/pstack-harness/agents/pstack-poteto-agent.md) · 2 copies)
- **pstack-reader**: Read-only delegate for pstack's explorers, investigators and reviewers. Spawn it where a pstack skill names `pstack-reader`. It has no file-editing tools, and the pstack hook denies its shell commands that write. ([EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc](https://github.com/EvanBatten/pstack-for-cc/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-reader.md) · ★2)
- **pstack-reader**: Read-only pstack worker for exploring, explaining, reviewing, and judging. Keeps MCP, web, and skills; has no edit or write tools. Use where a pstack skill asks for a readonly or read-only subagent. ([proguha/pstack-pi](https://github.com/proguha/pstack-pi/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-reader.md) · 2 copies)
- **pstack-readonly**: Read-only pstack worker for explorers, investigators, reviewers, and judges. Can read files and run inspection commands, cannot edit. Use wherever a pstack skill asks for readonly true. ([charlietlamb/cstack](https://github.com/charlietlamb/cstack/blob/HEAD/pstack/agents/pstack-readonly.md) · ★1)
- **pstack-readonly**: Read-only delegate for pstack skills that ask for Cursor's generalPurpose subagent with readonly true. Reads and searches, never edits. ([hoesler/agent-stuff](https://github.com/hoesler/agent-stuff/blob/HEAD/pi/extensions/pstack/pstack/docs/harness/agents/pstack-readonly.md))
- **pstack-readonly**: Read-only pstack worker for review panels, explorers, and explainers that skills such as `how` and `arena` spawn. Reads code and runs read-only commands. Never edits files. Has no MCP tools, so skills that need MCP evidence spawn `general-purpose` instead. ([RayenTellissy/pstack-claude](https://github.com/RayenTellissy/pstack-claude/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-readonly.md))
- **pstack-readonly**: Read-only explorer, investigator, reviewer, or judge for pstack skills. Reads code, searches, runs non-mutating commands, and reports findings. Never edits files. ([slappcard/pi-pstack-skills](https://github.com/slappcard/pi-pstack-skills/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-readonly.md))
- **pstack-readonly**: Read-only child for pstack runs that ask for `readonly: true`. Sees code, history, and tests; edits nothing. Use it when a Cursor skill says a child must be readonly, and say "do not edit files" in the brief anyway. Check git status afterward — read-only is a convention here, not an enforcement boundary. ([udit-001/pi-pstack](https://github.com/udit-001/pi-pstack/blob/HEAD/agents/pstack-readonly.md))

- **ptr-channel**: Use to run one coding package ("channel") for the PTR head in a dedicated git worktree. Trigger when the head hands over a package with a goal, a list of owned files and a base branch. Edits only the owned files, never commits, pushes, merges, tags or freezes, and hands back once with changed files, real command outputs and open points. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-channel.md) · ★1)
- **ptr-claims-auditor**: Use to audit PTR docs, READMEs, STATUS.md, component.toml text, PR descriptions or experiment result write-ups for over-claiming. Trigger before publishing or merging any text that says a component or model "improves", "outperforms", "specialises", "is implemented", "passes" or cites numbers, and when STATUS.md, docs/components/STATUS.md or research/novelty/ change. Read-only; reports verdicts and replacement wording, never edits. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-claims-auditor.md) · ★1)
- **ptr-experiment-designer**: Use to draft a falsifiable experiment design for a PTR research question, such as "do typed slots help hard-constraint retention" or "does verifier-precedence weak supervision calibrate better than majority vote". Trigger when someone proposes a new experiment, a successor to a prepared or inconclusive one, or asks how to test a claim from research/novelty/CLAIMS.md. Produces the design and the exact scripts/new_experiment.py command in its reply only; read-only, never creates the experiment. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-experiment-designer.md) · ★1)
- **ptr-fact-checker**: Use to fact-check changed PTR docs, STATUS files and write-ups against the recorded evidence. Trigger before a head merges documentation or a result write-up that cites numbers, verdicts, hashes, tags, seed lists, mutation counts or file:line locations. Read-only; reports a table of mismatches with severity and never edits. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-fact-checker.md) · ★1)
- **ptr-invariant-reviewer**: Use to review a diff, branch or PR in the PTR repository against the 17 binding invariants in docs/INVARIANTS.md, docs/COMPONENT_CONTRACTS.md and the CLAUDE.md conventions. Trigger before merging or opening a PR that touches crates/, bins/, proto/ or model/, especially changes to ptr-runtime, ptr-ledger, ptr-state, ptr-pg, ptr-search, ptr-security, ptr-exec, ptr-verifier, ptr-branch, ptr-fastmem or ptr-lineage, or when asked "does this violate an invariant?". Read-only; reports findings, never fixes them. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-invariant-reviewer.md) · ★1)
- **ptr-rereviewer**: Use for an adversarial, read-only re-review of one channel round in the PTR repository. Trigger after a channel hands back and before the head integrates, or when the head asks "is this round clean?". Reads the diff against the base, re-runs read-only and Python checks, and reports findings P0/P1/P2 with file:line and a verdict. Never edits. ([xrey167/PTR](https://github.com/xrey167/PTR/blob/HEAD/.claude/plugins/ptr-dev/agents/ptr-rereviewer.md) · ★1)
- **ptu-availability**: Looks up Azure PTU and PAYG Standard model availability by running the installed `ptu availability` command. Use when the user asks whether a model is available in a region, wants the default PTU availability report, or asks to compare Regional, Data Zone, or Global availability. Uses the installed tool's active preset and defaults when the user gives no filters. ([sujithq/ptu](https://github.com/sujithq/ptu/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/ptu-availability.agent.md))
- **ptu-availability-direct**: Independently checks Azure PTU and PAYG Standard availability without running the installed `ptu` tool. Use when the user wants an independent availability lookup, asks to verify a ptu CLI result, or requests the same default report through the underlying data sources. Uses the active preset or factory defaults recorded by the ptu project. ([sujithq/ptu](https://github.com/sujithq/ptu/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/ptu-availability-direct.agent.md))
- **pty-expert**: Expert on the Unix process lifecycle on macOS — forkpty, exec, process groups, signals, waitpid, descriptors. Call whenever spawning, stopping, restarting, death detection or PTY draining is involved. This is vigile's most treacherous area: the bugs there are silent and only show up hours later. ([kevinbarfleur/vigile](https://github.com/kevinbarfleur/vigile/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/pty-expert.md))
